## GHSA-f3w6-w445-fwh9 · activestorage

2026-11-12 · Ruby · **CRITICAL** 9.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The ImageMagick library, used by Active Storage for image analysis, has known vulnerabilities that allow for arbitrary file operations when processing untrusted image files. Without a strict security policy, an attacker could craft malicious image files to exploit these vulnerabilities.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary file write and read capabilities on the server, potentially leading to remote code execution, sensitive data exfiltration, or denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- /dev/null
+++ b/activestorage/lib/active_storage/image_magick.rb
@@ -0,0 +1,63 @@
+module ActiveStorage
+  module ImageMagick
+    POLICY_DIRECTORY = File.expand_path("image_magick", __dir__)
+    def self.apply_policy # :nodoc:
+      MiniMagick.cli_env = MiniMagick.cli_env.merge("MAGICK_CONFIGURE_PATH" => configure_path(operator_paths)).freeze
+    end
+  end
+end
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `active_storage/image_magick.rb` file that applies a security policy to all ImageMagick processes spawned by MiniMagick. It sets the `MAGICK_CONFIGURE_PATH` environment variable to include a directory containing a restrictive `policy.xml` file, mitigating known ImageMagick vulnerabilities. It also ensures that a securable version of MiniMagick (5.1 or later) is used.

## GHSA-4g86-54fm-h9h8 · Aspire.Dashboard.Sdk.win-arm64

2026-11-12 · C# · **CRITICAL** 9.1 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The Aspire Dashboard did not consistently enforce authentication checks across all entry points and components. Specifically, some components like MainLayout and DashboardDialogProvider were not ensuring the user was authenticated before proceeding with their initialization logic, allowing unauthenticated access to parts of the dashboard.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass the authentication mechanism and gain unauthorized access to the Aspire Dashboard, potentially viewing sensitive application data or performing actions they are not authorized to do.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Aspire.Dashboard/Components/Layout/MainLayout.razor.cs
+++ b/src/Aspire.Dashboard/Components/Layout/MainLayout.razor.cs
@@ -104,10 +106,15 @@ public partial class MainLayout : IGlobalKeydownListener, IAsyncDisposable
     [CascadingParameter]
     public required ViewportInformation ViewportInformation { get; set; }
 
+    [CascadingParameter]
+    public Task<AuthenticationState>? AuthenticationState { get; set; }
+
     private bool IsTerminalDockEnabled => !_isSwitchingRuns && DashboardClient.IsEnabled && !DashboardClient.IsReadOnly;
 
     protected override async Task OnInitializedAsync()
     {
+        await DashboardAuthenticationState.EnsureAuthenticatedAsync(Options.CurrentValue, AuthenticationState);
+
         if (RunStore.SupportsRunSelection)
         {
             var selectedRunResult = await SessionStorage.GetAsync<string>(BrowserStorageKeys.SelectedDashboardRunId);
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new component, DashboardNotAuthorized, to handle unauthorized access and redirects users to the login page if BrowserToken authentication is enabled. Crucially, it adds explicit calls to `DashboardAuthenticationState.EnsureAuthenticatedAsync` in `MainLayout.razor.cs` and `DashboardDialogProvider.cs` to enforce authentication checks at the start of component initialization.

## GHSA-vq9p-w6wv-cr25 · github.com/pgEdge/control-plane

2026-11-12 · Go · **HIGH** 7.8 · Pattern: `INSECURE_DEFAULT→CONFIG`

### Root cause

The pgEdge Control Plane generated pg_hba.conf files with 'trust' authentication for local and loopback connections. This allowed any user connecting locally or via loopback to authenticate without a password, effectively gaining superuser privileges if they could access the database.

### Impact

An attacker with local or loopback network access to the database could bypass authentication and gain full control over the PostgreSQL instance, including reading, modifying, or deleting any data.

### Key diff

```
-			AuthMethod: hba.AuthMethodTrust,
+			AuthMethod:  hba.AuthMethodPeer,
+			AuthOptions: "map=local_superuser",
```

### Fix summary

The patch removes the 'trust' authentication method for local and loopback connections in pg_hba.conf. It replaces it with 'peer' authentication for the 'pgedge' user via a map and explicitly rejects connections for system users ('pgedge', 'patroni_replicator') over local and non-SSL host connections, enforcing certificate-based authentication for system roles.

## GHSA-73v3-wf97-vpgj · msgpack

2026-11-12 · Ruby · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The JRuby decoder for msgpack-ruby would pre-allocate arrays and byte buffers based on header-specified sizes without validating these sizes against the actual remaining buffer capacity. An attacker could send a small message with a large size header, causing the application to attempt to allocate a multi-gigabyte array or byte buffer.

### Impact

An attacker could trigger an OutOfMemoryError (OOM) by sending a specially crafted, small message, leading to a denial of service for the application processing the message.

### Key diff

```
--- a/ext/java/org/msgpack/jruby/Decoder.java
+++ b/ext/java/org/msgpack/jruby/Decoder.java
@@ -137,11 +137,15 @@ private IRubyObject consumeString(int size, Encoding encoding) {
   }
 
   private IRubyObject consumeArray(int size) {
-    IRubyObject[] elements = new IRubyObject[size];
+    int preallocatedSize = size;
+    if (preallocatedSize > buffer.remaining()) {
+      preallocatedSize = buffer.remaining();
+    }
+    RubyArray array = runtime.newArray(preallocatedSize);
     for (int i = 0; i < size; i++) {
-      elements[i] = next();
+      array.push(next());
     }
-    return runtime.newArray(elements);
+    return array;
   }
 
   private IRubyObject consumeHash(int size) {
@@ -184,9 +188,12 @@ private IRubyObject consumeExtension(int size) {
   }
 
   private byte[] readBytes(int size) {
-    byte[] payload = new byte[size];
-    buffer.get(payload);
-    return payload;
+    if (buffer.remaining() >= size) {
+      byte[] payload = new byte[size];
+      buffer.get(payload);
+      return payload;
+    }
+    throw new BufferUnderflowException();
   }
 
   @Override
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces checks to limit array pre-allocation and byte buffer reading to the actual remaining capacity of the input buffer. If the requested size exceeds the available buffer, it either limits the pre-allocation to the buffer's remaining size or throws a BufferUnderflowException.

## GHSA-wm3x-v3qw-4jh3 · actionpack

2026-11-12 · Ruby · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `TYPE_CONFUSION→BYPASS`

### Root cause

The `each_key` method was delegated directly to the underlying `@parameters` hash, which holds all parameters, including unpermitted ones. This allowed an attacker to iterate over all parameter keys, effectively bypassing the strong parameters mechanism designed to filter out unpermitted attributes during mass assignment.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass strong parameter protections, leading to mass assignment vulnerabilities where unpermitted attributes (e.g., `admin: true`) could be set on a model, potentially escalating privileges or altering data unexpectedly.

### Key diff

```
--- a/actionpack/lib/action_controller/metal/strong_parameters.rb
+++ b/actionpack/lib/action_controller/metal/strong_parameters.rb
@@ -248,7 +247,7 @@ class Parameters
     # Returns the content of the parameters as a string.
 
     delegate :keys, :empty?, :exclude?, :include?,
-      :as_json, :to_s, :each_key, to: :@parameters
+      :as_json, :to_s, to: :@parameters
 
     alias_method :has_key?, :include?
     alias_method :key?, :include?
@@ -420,6 +419,15 @@ def each_value(&block)
       self
     end
 
+    # Calls block once for each key in the parameters, passing the key. If no block
+    # is given, an enumerator is returned instead.
+    def each_key(&block)
+      return to_enum(:each_key) unless block_given?
+      @parameters.each_key(&block)
+
+      self
+    end
```

### Fix summary

The `each_key` method is no longer delegated directly to the internal `@parameters` hash. Instead, it is now explicitly defined within the `Parameters` class, ensuring that it operates within the strong parameters context and does not expose unpermitted keys directly.

## GHSA-h6v6-rg66-fqj4 · loofah

2026-11-12 · Ruby · **MODERATE** 6.1 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The Loofah gem, when scrubbing HTML5, did not properly escape attribute values, particularly when using the Nokogiri HTML5 parser (Gumbo). This allowed an attacker to inject malicious JavaScript into the output by crafting specific HTML attributes that would be unescaped and executed by the browser.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary JavaScript execution in the context of the user's browser, leading to session hijacking, defacement, or redirection to malicious sites.

### Key diff

```
-	  spec.add_dependency("nokogiri", [">= 1.12.0"])
+	  spec.add_dependency("nokogiri", [">= 1.19.5"])
```

### Fix summary

The patch updates the minimum required Nokogiri version to 1.19.5. This new Nokogiri version includes the necessary fixes for handling HTML5 attribute escaping, specifically addressing the mutation XSS vulnerability when using the Gumbo parser.

## GHSA-v6rj-xm57-3p59 · actionview

2026-11-12 · Ruby · **MODERATE** 6.1 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The `highlight` helper in Action View did not properly HTML escape the matched phrases when using a custom `highlighter` string. This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary HTML or JavaScript if they could control the `text` or `phrases` input, leading to Cross-Site Scripting.

### Impact

An attacker could inject malicious client-side scripts into the rendered HTML, potentially leading to session hijacking, defacement, or redirection of users to malicious sites.

### Key diff

```
-                segment.gsub!(pattern, highlighter)
+                segment.gsub!(segment_pattern) do
+                  match = Regexp.last_match
+                  highlighter.gsub(/\(\d)/) { ERB::Util.html_escape_once(match[$1.to_i]) }
+                end
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `highlight` helper to explicitly HTML escape the matched phrases before substituting them into the `highlighter` string. It also adds a warning to the documentation about safe usage of placeholders in different HTML contexts.

## GHSA-rwvh-fw6r-pwvp · actionpack

2026-11-12 · Ruby · **MODERATE** 5.9 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from the `ActionController::Live` streaming mechanism not properly handling a `ClosedQueueError` during the `write` operation. If the underlying buffer (`@buf`) was closed by a concurrent thread (e.g., due to a client disconnecting), a subsequent `write` call would raise a `ClosedQueueError` which was not caught, leading to the application crashing and potentially causing a DoS.

### Impact

An attacker could trigger application crashes by causing the client to disconnect prematurely during a live stream, leading to a denial of service for other users of the application.

### Key diff

```
--- a/actionpack/lib/action_controller/metal/live.rb
+++ b/actionpack/lib/action_controller/metal/live.rb
@@ -218,7 +218,11 @@ def write(string)
           @response.delete_header "Content-Length"
         end
 
-        super
+        begin
+          super
+        rescue ClosedQueueError
+          raise IOError, "closed stream" if connected?
+        end
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces error handling for `ClosedQueueError` within the `write` method, converting it into an `IOError` if the connection is still active. Additionally, the `close` and `abort` methods now explicitly close the internal buffer (`@buf.close`) and broadcast to waiting threads, ensuring proper state management and preventing race conditions that could lead to the `ClosedQueueError`.

## GHSA-68g8-gh8f-7rhg · github.com/pgEdge/pgedge-rag-server

2026-11-12 · Go · **MODERATE** 5.8 · Pattern: `INFO_DISCLOSURE→ERROR_MESSAGE`

### Root cause

The application was directly relaying error messages from upstream LLM providers to API clients. Some LLM providers, upon authentication failure, include a truncated form of the submitted API key within their error response bodies. Since the affected API endpoints were unauthenticated, this allowed any caller to potentially receive part of the configured provider credential.

### Impact

An attacker could obtain partial credentials (e.g., a truncated API key) for the configured LLM provider by triggering an authentication failure or other errors, even without authentication to the RAG server itself.

### Key diff

```
--- a/internal/server/handlers.go
+++ b/internal/server/handlers.go
@@ -127,7 +128,11 @@ func (s *Server) handlePipeline(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
 				"pipeline not found: "+name)
 			return
 		}
-		s.respondError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, "INTERNAL_ERROR", err.Error())
+		s.logger.Error("failed to resolve pipeline",
+			"pipeline", name,
+			"error", safeerr.RedactError(err))
+		s.respondError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, "INTERNAL_ERROR",
+			safeerr.MsgInternal)
 		return
 	}
```

### Fix summary

A new `safeerr` package was introduced to classify internal errors and generate client-safe descriptions, preventing raw provider error messages from being exposed. Additionally, a `Redact` function was added to scrub credential-shaped strings from error messages before logging them, providing defense in depth for logs.

## GHSA-g6f8-c3x9-jv7f · code16/sharp

2026-11-12 · PHP · **MODERATE** 5.4 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The application allowed an unanchored regular expression match for internal route validation, meaning an attacker could craft a URL that partially matched a legitimate internal endpoint and then append arbitrary paths. This, combined with the ability to specify the HTTP method, allowed an attacker to bypass the intended endpoint validation and make requests to internal resources.

### Impact

An attacker could perform Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF) to access internal network resources or potentially trigger actions on internal APIs that were not intended to be exposed.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Http/Controllers/Api/ApiFormAutocompleteController.php
+++ b/src/Http/Controllers/Api/ApiFormAutocompleteController.php
@@ -89,25 +91,22 @@ private function normalizeEndpoint(string $input): string
         return str($input)->startsWith('http') ? $input : url($input);
     }
 
-    private function checkEndpoint(string $requestEndpoint, string $fieldEndpoint): void
+    private function checkEndpoint(string $requestEndpoint, string $fieldEndpoint, string $fieldMethod): void
     {
         // Validates that the endpoint defined in the field is the same as the one called
         preg_match(
-            '#'
+            '#^'
             .str()
                 ->of(preg_quote($fieldEndpoint))
                 ->replaceMatches('#\\{\\{(.*)\\}\\}#', '(.*)')
-            .'#im',
+            .'$#im',
             $requestEndpoint
         ) ?: throw new SharpInvalidConfigException('The endpoint is not the one defined in the autocomplete field.');
 
-        // Check that the remote route exists in the app
-        collect(Route::getRoutes()->getRoutes())
-            ->map(fn ($route) => url($route->uri))
-            ->filter(fn (string $routeUrl) => preg_match(
-                '#'.str()->of($routeUrl)->replaceMatches('#\{(.*)\}#', '(.*)').'#im',
-                str($requestEndpoint)->before('?')
-            ))
-            ->count() > 0 ?: throw new SharpInvalidConfigException('The endpoint is not a valid internal route.');
+        try {
+            Route::getRoutes()->match(Request::create($requestEndpoint, method: $fieldMethod));
+        } catch (NotFoundHttpException|MethodNotAllowedHttpException $e) {
+            throw new SharpInvalidConfigException('The endpoint is not a valid internal route.', previous: $e);
+        }
     }
```

### Fix summary

The patch anchors the regular expression used for endpoint validation to ensure an exact match from the beginning to the end of the string. Additionally, it now uses Laravel's route matching mechanism, including the HTTP method, to rigorously validate if the requested endpoint is a truly valid and accessible internal route.

## GHSA-h24v-f482-8cwf · code16/sharp

2026-11-12 · PHP · **MODERATE** 5.4 · Pattern: `DESERIALIZATION→OBJECT_INJECTION`

### Root cause

The application allowed an attacker to control the class name instantiated via the `embed_key` API parameter. The `Str::replace('.', '\', $embedKey)` call converted dots to backslashes, which could then be used to reference any class within the application's autoloader, leading to arbitrary object creation.

### Impact

An attacker could instantiate arbitrary classes, potentially leading to denial of service, information disclosure, or even remote code execution if a gadget chain involving a vulnerable class was present and could be triggered by its constructor or methods.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Http/Controllers/Api/Embeds/HandlesEmbed.php
+++ b/src/Http/Controllers/Api/Embeds/HandlesEmbed.php
@@ -9,7 +9,13 @@ trait HandlesEmbed
 {
     protected function getEmbedFromKey(string $embedKey): SharpFormEditorEmbed
     {
-        $embed = app(Str::replace('.', '\', $embedKey));
+        $embedClass = Str::replace('.', '\', $embedKey);
+
+        if (! is_a($embedClass, SharpFormEditorEmbed::class, true)) {
+            throw new \Exception("Embed class $embedClass is not a SharpFormEditorEmbed");
+        }
+
+        $embed = app($embedClass);
         $embed->buildEmbedConfig();
 
         return $embed;
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds a check to ensure that the class derived from the `embedKey` parameter is a subclass of `SharpFormEditorEmbed`. If it's not, an exception is thrown, preventing the instantiation of arbitrary classes.

## GHSA-4h7h-rv7c-7m2v · hono

2026-11-12 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 5.3 · Pattern: `UNCLASSIFIED`

### Root cause

The regular expression for `multipart/form-data` content type was too restrictive, specifically expecting only a 'boundary' parameter. If additional parameters were present in the `Content-Type` header (e.g., `multipart/form-data; charset=UTF-8; boundary=...`), the regex would fail to match, causing the validator to skip validation for the request body.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass form data validation by including extra, benign parameters in the `Content-Type` header of a `multipart/form-data` request. This could lead to the processing of unvalidated input, potentially enabling other vulnerabilities if the application logic relies solely on this validator.

### Key diff

```
-const multipartRegex = /^multipart\/form-data(;\s?boundary=[a-zA-Z0-9'"()+_,\-./:=?]+)?$/i
+const multipartRegex = /^multipart\/form-data(;\s*[a-zA-Z0-9\-]+\=([^;]+))*$/i
```

### Fix summary

The patch updates the regular expression for `multipart/form-data` to correctly handle additional parameters in the `Content-Type` header. It now allows for any number of key-value pair parameters after the initial `multipart/form-data` declaration, ensuring that validation is always applied when intended.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 1084 occurrences**
> Languages: JavaScript, Go, Java, PHP, Python, C#, YAML, RUST, Erlang, Ruby, Swift, Dart
> → GHSA-jvff-x2qm-6286
> → GHSA-8jvc-mcx6-r4cg

## GHSA-7m8x-x364-m26j · signalk-server

2026-11-12 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 5.3 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The Signal K server allowed unauthenticated WebSocket clients to subscribe to the 'log' path, which exposed buffered server logs without proper authorization checks. The application's security strategy was not enforced for this specific subscription.

### Impact

An unauthenticated attacker could gain access to sensitive server logs, potentially revealing internal system information, debugging details, or other data that should be restricted to administrators.

### Key diff

```
-    msg.subscribe.length > 0 &&
-    msg.subscribe[0].path === 'log'
+    msg.subscribe.length > 0 &&
+    msg.subscribe[0].path === 'log'
+  ) {
+    if (
+      !app.securityStrategy.isDummy() &&
+      !app.securityStrategy.hasAdminAccess?.(spark.request)
+    ) {
+      debug('server log subscription denied: admin access required')
+      spark.write({
+        errorMessage: 'Server log access requires admin permissions'
+      })
+      return
+    }
     if (!spark.logUnsubscribe) {
       spark.logUnsubscribe = startServerLog(app, spark)
     }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces an authorization check for 'log' subscriptions. It verifies if the client has administrative access using `app.securityStrategy.hasAdminAccess` before allowing the subscription. If admin access is not present, the subscription is denied, and an error message is sent to the client.

## GHSA-r7hq-q2xm-6c5m · @grpc/grpc-js-xds

2026-11-12 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 4.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→REGEX`

### Root cause

The `safe_regex` library, used for matching regular expressions, incorrectly handled patterns containing alternation (`|`) when they were implicitly anchored with `^` and `$` without being wrapped in a capturing group. This allowed partial matches to succeed where a full string match was intended, leading to potential bypasses or unexpected behavior.

### Impact

An attacker could craft specific input strings that would bypass the intended regular expression matching logic, potentially leading to unauthorized access or other security policy violations if these regexes were used for validation or access control.

### Key diff

```
this.targetRegexImpl = new RE2(`^${targetRegex}$`, 'u');
-    this.targetRegexImpl = new RE2(`^(${targetRegex})$`, 'u');
+    this.targetRegexImpl = new RE2(`^(${targetRegex})$`, 'u');
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the regular expression construction by wrapping the user-provided `targetRegex` in a non-capturing group `()` before anchoring it with `^` and `$` characters. This ensures that the entire provided regex is treated as a single unit for matching the full string.

## GHSA-3w93-6hmw-whh4 · activestorage

2026-11-12 · Ruby · **MODERATE** 4.7 · Pattern: `UNCLASSIFIED`

### Root cause

The GCS direct upload mechanism, when using V2 signatures, did not include the `Cache-Control` header in the signed request. This allowed an attacker to modify the `Cache-Control` header after the URL was signed but before the upload was completed, as it was not cryptographically protected.

### Impact

An attacker could manipulate the `Cache-Control` header of directly uploaded files, potentially leading to cache poisoning or denial-of-service by forcing clients or proxies to cache malicious content for extended periods.

### Key diff

```
--- a/activestorage/lib/active_storage/service/gcs_service.rb
+++ b/activestorage/lib/active_storage/service/gcs_service.rb
-        if @config[:cache_control].present?
-          headers["Cache-Control"] = @config[:cache_control]
-          version = :v4
-        end
-        headers.merge!(custom_metadata_headers(custom_metadata))
+        # V2 signatures bind the Content-Type (empty) and Content-MD5 headers. GCS leaves headers
+        # other than x-goog-* out of V2 signatures, so the client sends Cache-Control unsigned.
         args = {
           content_md5: checksum,
           expires: expires_in,
-          headers: headers,
+          headers: custom_metadata_headers(custom_metadata),
           method: "PUT",
-          version: version,
+          version: :v2,
         }
```

### Fix summary

The patch removes the logic that conditionally switched to V4 signing for `Cache-Control` and instead ensures that `Cache-Control` is not included in the V2 signed headers. This prevents the header from being tampered with, as GCS V2 signatures only bind `Content-Type` and `Content-MD5` by default, and other headers like `Cache-Control` are not signed unless explicitly prefixed with `x-goog-`.

## GHSA-m3x9-938q-5767 · loofah

2026-11-12 · Ruby · **MODERATE** 4.7 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an incomplete sanitization of CSS `url()` values. Specifically, the previous sanitization logic failed to properly handle escaped parentheses within `url()` constructs, allowing an attacker to bypass the filter and inject malicious CSS that could lead to XSS.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary CSS, potentially leading to Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) attacks by crafting malicious `url()` values that execute JavaScript in the victim's browser when the sanitized content is rendered.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/loofah/html5/scrub.rb
+++ b/lib/loofah/html5/scrub.rb
@@ -124,15 +125,12 @@ def scrub_css_attribute(node)
         end
 
         def scrub_css(style)
-          url_flags = [:url, :bad_url]
           style_tree = Crass.parse_properties(style)
           sanitized_tree = []
 
           style_tree.each do |node|
             next unless node[:node] == :property
-            next if node[:children].any? do |child|
-              url_flags.include?(child[:node])
-            end
+            next if node[:children].any? { |child| CSS_URLISH_NODES.include?(child[:node]) }
 
             name = node[:name].downcase
             next unless SafeList::ALLOWED_CSS_PROPERTIES.include?(name) ||
@@ -156,7 +154,7 @@ def scrub_css(style)
                 if !SafeList::SHORTHAND_CSS_PROPERTIES.include?(name.split("-").first) ||
                     SafeList::ALLOWED_CSS_KEYWORDS.include?(keyword) ||
                     (keyword =~ CSS_KEYWORDISH)
-                  keyword
+                  child[:raw]
                 end
               else
                 child[:raw]
@@ -166,14 +164,39 @@ def scrub_css(style)
             next if value.empty?
 
             value << CSS_WHITESPACE << CSS_IMPORTANT if node[:important]
+            # Dropping tokens can join the survivors into new ones (`url/(a)*/(b)` becomes `url/*/`),
+            # so the rebuilt string may tokenize differently from the original and stringify
+            # differently again. Keep a property only if its rebuilt string survives a parse and
+            # stringify unchanged, so the tokens checked are exactly the tokens a browser will see.
             propstring = format("%s:%s", name, value)
             sanitized_node = Crass.parse_properties(propstring).first
+            next unless Crass::Parser.stringify(sanitized_node) == propstring
+            next if css_property_unsafe?(sanitized_node)
+
             sanitized_tree << sanitized_node << CRASS_SEMICOLON
           end
 
           Crass::Parser.stringify(sanitized_tree)
         end
 
+        # Dropping a token joins its neighbours, which can build a token that was not in the input:
+        # dropping the string from `url\28 "x"linear-gradient(red)` leaves one function token. So the
+        # rebuilt property is checked for functions and strings, which the input check never sees.
+        def css_property_unsafe?(node)
+          node[:children].any? do |child|
+            case child[:node]
+            when *CSS_URLISH_NODES
+              true
+            when :string
+              !CSS_PROPERTY_STRING_WITHOUT_EMBEDDED_QUOTES.match?(child[:raw])
+            when :function
+              !SafeList::ALLOWED_CSS_FUNCTIONS.include?(child[:name].downcase(:ascii))
+            else
+              false
+            end
+          end
+        end
+
         def scrub_attribute_that_allows_local_ref(attr_node)
           return unless attr_node.value
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a more robust sanitization process for CSS properties. It now re-parses and re-stringifies the sanitized CSS property to ensure that no new unsafe tokens are formed by the removal of other tokens. Additionally, it explicitly checks for `bad_string` nodes and re-validates strings and functions after the initial sanitization to prevent bypasses via escaped characters.

## GHSA-gg5j-6742-v827 · @grpc/grpc-js-xds

2026-11-12 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The xDS client in @grpc/grpc-js-xds incorrectly used the filter's typeUrl instead of its name to store override configurations. This meant that RBAC (Role-Based Access Control) override rules defined in VirtualHost and Route resources were not correctly applied, leading to a situation where the default RBAC configuration was always used, even when more specific overrides were intended.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially bypass intended access restrictions by sending requests that should have been blocked by specific RBAC rules defined at the VirtualHost or Route level. This could lead to unauthorized access to resources or actions.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/grpc-js-xds/src/server.ts
+++ b/packages/grpc-js-xds/src/server.ts
@@ -170,7 +170,7 @@ class FilterChainEntry {
       const filterConfig = parseTopLevelFilterConfig(filter.typed_config!, false);
       if (filterConfig) {
         const filterOverrideConfigMap = new Map<string, HttpFilterConfig>();
-        this.overrideConfigMaps.set(filterConfig.typeUrl, filterOverrideConfigMap);
+        this.overrideConfigMaps.set(filter.name, filterOverrideConfigMap);
         const filterInterceptor = createServerHttpFilter(filterConfig, filterOverrideConfigMap);
         if (filterInterceptor) {
           httpFilterInterceptors.push(filterInterceptor);
```

### Fix summary

The patch corrects the key used for storing filter override configurations from `typeUrl` to `filter.name`. It also ensures that VirtualHost-level RBAC overrides are correctly processed and that Route-level overrides, if present, take precedence over VirtualHost overrides, thereby correctly applying the intended access control policies.

## GHSA-jcff-cj73-4w4f · loofah

2026-11-12 · Ruby · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The `allowed_uri?` method in Loofah did not correctly decode all HTML5 named character references for URI delimiters, specifically `&sol;` for `/`. This allowed an attacker to bypass the URI sanitization logic by encoding the slash in `data:text/html` as `&sol;`, making the sanitizer incorrectly treat it as `data:text&sol;html` (a safe `text/plain` type) instead of `data:text/html` (an unsafe `text/html` type).

### Impact

An attacker could inject malicious HTML content, including scripts, into a web page by crafting a `data:` URI that bypasses Loofah's sanitization, leading to Cross-Site Scripting (XSS).

### Key diff

```
-	uri_string.gsub!("&colon;", ":")
+	DELIMITER_CHARACTER_REFERENCES = { "&colon;" => ":", "&sol;" => "/" }.freeze
+	DELIMITER_CHARACTER_REFERENCE = Regexp.union(DELIMITER_CHARACTER_REFERENCES.keys)
...
+	uri_string.gsub!(DELIMITER_CHARACTER_REFERENCE, DELIMITER_CHARACTER_REFERENCES)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new constant `DELIMITER_CHARACTER_REFERENCES` to explicitly map `&colon;` and `&sol;` to their respective characters. It then modifies the `allowed_uri?` method to use a regular expression `DELIMITER_CHARACTER_REFERENCE` to replace all such named character references with their actual characters before further processing, ensuring correct URI parsing.

## GHSA-wqgw-9xfh-qf2g · Pillow

2026-11-12 · Python · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The TIFF and EXIF IFD parsing logic in Pillow did not properly validate the size of tag data against the total file size. An attacker could craft a TIFF file with overlapping tag data or tag data that collectively exceeded the file's actual size, leading to excessive memory allocation when Pillow attempted to read these oversized or overlapping data blocks.

### Impact

An attacker could supply a specially crafted TIFF file to an application using Pillow, causing the application to consume an inordinate amount of memory and potentially crash or become unresponsive, leading to a denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/PIL/TiffImagePlugin.py
+++ b/src/PIL/TiffImagePlugin.py
@@ -902,6 +905,7 @@ def load(self, fp: IO[bytes]) -> None:
                 if self._bigtiff
                 else self._unpack("H", self._ensure_read(fp, 2))
             )[0]
+            total_size = 0
             for i in range(tag_count):
                 tag, typ, count, data = (
                     self._unpack("HHQ8s", self._ensure_read(fp, 20))
@@ -928,6 +932,12 @@ def load(self, fp: IO[bytes]) -> None:
                     if offset >= 2**63:
                         warnings.warn("Tag offset too large")
                         continue
+                    # Well-formed IFD values should fit within the stream they
+                    # are read from, even before accounting for overlaps.
+                    total_size += size
+                    if total_size > file_size:
+                        warnings.warn(f"Tag data larger than file. Skipping tag {tag}")
+                        continue
                     fp.seek(offset)
                     data = ImageFile._safe_read(fp, size)
                     fp.seek(here)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a check to calculate the total size of tag data being read. It compares this accumulated size against the actual file size. If the total tag data size exceeds the file size, a warning is issued, and the problematic tag is skipped, preventing excessive memory allocation.

## GHSA-xcvg-w56q-r89m · Pillow

2026-11-12 · Python · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `BUFFER_OVERFLOW→HEAP`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the `TIFFWriteScanline` function, used to write image data, would read a scanline based on the image dimensions specified in TIFF tags (IMAGEWIDTH, SAMPLESPERPIXEL, BITSPERSAMPLE). If these tags described a row larger than the actual buffer allocated for a single row (`state->buffer`), it would lead to a heap buffer overflow.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious TIFF file that, when processed, would cause a heap buffer overflow. This could lead to a denial of service (application crash) or potentially arbitrary code execution.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/libImaging/TiffDecode.c
+++ b/src/libImaging/TiffDecode.c
@@ -820,6 +820,14 @@ ImagingLibTiffEncode(Imaging im, ImagingCodecState state, UINT8 *buffer, int byt
     TIFF *tiff = clientstate->tiff;
 
     if (state->state == 0) {
+        if (TIFFScanlineSize64(tiff) > (uint64_t)state->bytes) {
+            state->errcode = IMAGING_CODEC_CONFIG;
+            return -1;
+        }
+
         // Encoding line by line
         while (state->y < state->ysize) {
             state->shuffle(
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds a check before encoding each line. It compares the expected scanline size reported by `TIFFScanlineSize64` with the size of the allocated buffer (`state->bytes`). If the TIFF tags indicate a larger scanline than the buffer can hold, an error (`IMAGING_CODEC_CONFIG`) is set, preventing the `TIFFWriteScanline` call and thus the buffer overflow.
